\title{Product and factorial formulas for refined Bell numbers}
\author{}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\section*{Definitions}
\paragraph{Bell.} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:1}\paperlineanchor{defeq:1}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:1}{D1})} Let \(m\) be a multiset of elements of \(\mathbb{N}\). Then \(\operatorname{bell}(m) = \operatorname{multinomial}(\operatorname{to\,Finset}(m),\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1}))\).

\paragraph{Uniform bell.} \par\noindent\refstepcounter{definingequation}\label{defeq:2}\paperlineanchor{defeq:2}\textup{(\paperlink{defeq:2}{D2})} Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) = \operatorname{bell}(\operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))\).

\subsection*{Prerequisites}
\begin{description}
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:416e642e6c656674}\paperlink{premise:416e642e6c656674}{P1.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:416e642e6c656674}\textbf{Left.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be propositions. Assume \(a \land b\). Then \(a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e6d756c74697365745f70726f645f706f73}\paperlink{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e6d756c74697365745f70726f645f706f73}{P2.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e6d756c74697365745f70726f645f706f73}\textbf{Multiset product positivity.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{No\,Zero\,Divisors}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Nontrivial}\left(R\right)\). Let \(m\) be a multiset of elements of \(R\). Then \(0 < \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(m) \iff (\forall x\, :\, R,\; x \in m \implies 0 < x)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f4578697374734164644f664c45}\paperlink{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f4578697374734164644f664c45}{P3.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f4578697374734164644f664c45}\textbf{To exists sum of at most.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{self}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Exists\,Add\,Of\,LE}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f5a65726f4c654f6e65436c617373}\paperlink{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f5a65726f4c654f6e65436c617373}{P4.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f5a65726f4c654f6e65436c617373}\textbf{To zero at most one class.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Zero\,Class}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Zero\,LEOne\,Class}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{P5.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}\textbf{Erase equals of not membership.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \notin s\). Then \(s \setminus \{a\} = s\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f73696e676c65746f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f73696e676c65746f6e}{P6.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f73696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Erase singleton.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\{a\} \setminus \{a\} = \varnothing\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}{P7.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}\textbf{Membership erase.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \in s \setminus \{b\} \iff a \ne b \land a \in s\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}{P8.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Membership singleton.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(b \in \{a\} \iff b = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f656d707479}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f656d707479}{P9.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f656d707479}\textbf{Not membership empty.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \notin \varnothing\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{P10.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}\textbf{Equality of products with equal factors.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\iota\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(f\) and \(g\) be functions of type \(\iota \to M\). Assume \(s_{1} = s_{2}\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \iota,\; x \in s_{2} \implies f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(\prod_{i\in s_{1}}\left(f(i)\right) = \prod_{i\in s_{2}}\left(g(i)\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}{P11.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}\textbf{Product constant one.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(\beta\right)\). Then \(\prod_{x\in s}\left(1\right) = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f656d707479}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f656d707479}{P12.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f656d707479}\textbf{Product empty.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(\beta\right)\). Then \(\prod_{x\in \varnothing}\left(f\left(x\right)\right) = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f65726173655f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f65726173655f6d756c}{P13.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f65726173655f6d756c}\textbf{Product erase product.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Assume equality on \(\iota\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\iota\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\iota \to M\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\iota\). Assume \(a \in s\). Then \(\prod_{x\in s \setminus \{a\}}(f(x)) \cdot f(a) = \prod_{x\in s}(f(x))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c5f64697374726962}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c5f64697374726962}{P14.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c5f64697374726962}\textbf{Product product distrib.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\iota\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(f\) and \(g\) be functions of type \(\iota \to M\). Then \(\prod_{x\in s}(f(x) \cdot g(x)) = \prod_{x\in s}(f(x)) \cdot \prod_{x\in s}(g(x))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c74697365745f6d61705f636f756e74}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c74697365745f6d61705f636f756e74}{P15.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c74697365745f6d61705f636f756e74}\textbf{Product multiset map count.} Let \(\iota\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\iota\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a multiset of elements of \(\iota\). Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\iota \to M\). Then \(\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(f,\allowbreak{} s)) = \prod_{m\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(s)}(f(m)^{\operatorname{count}(m,\allowbreak{} s)})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f706f73}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f706f73}{P16.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f706f73}\textbf{Product positivity.} Let \(\iota\) and \(R\) be types. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid\,With\,Zero}\left(R\right)\). Equip \(R\) with a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Zero\,LEOne\,Class}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Pos\,Mul\,Strict\,Mono}\left(R\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Nontrivial}\left(R\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\iota \to R\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\iota\). Assume \(\forall i\, :\, \iota,\; i \in s \implies 0 < f\left(i\right)\). Then \(0 < \prod_{i\in s}\left(f\left(i\right)\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f72616e67655f73756363}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f72616e67655f73756363}{P17.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f72616e67655f73756363}\textbf{Product range successor.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\mathbb{N} \to M\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\prod_{0\le x<n + 1}(f(x)) = \prod_{0\le x<n}(f(x)) \cdot f(n)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}{P18.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Product singleton.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\iota \to M\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\iota\). Then \(\prod_{x\in \{a\}}\left(f\left(x\right)\right) = f\left(a\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}{P19.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Equality of sums with equal summands.} Let \(\iota\) and \(M\) be types. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\iota\). Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(f\) and \(g\) be functions of type \(\iota \to M\). Assume \(s_{1} = s_{2}\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \iota,\; x \in s_{2} \implies f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(\sum_{i\in s_{1}}\left(f(i)\right) = \sum_{i\in s_{2}}\left(g(i)\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f6d756c74697365745f636f756e74}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f6d756c74697365745f636f756e74}{P20.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f6d756c74697365745f636f756e74}\textbf{Sum multiset count.} Let \(M\) be a type. Equip \(M\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Assume equality on \(M\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a multiset of elements of \(M\). Then \(\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(s) = \sum_{m\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(s)}(\sum_{k=1}^{\operatorname{count}(m,\allowbreak{} s)}(m))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}\paperlink{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}{P21.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}\textbf{To is left cancel sum.} Let \(G\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Add}\left(G\right)\). Let \(\mathit{self}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Left\,Cancel\,Add}\left(G\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745265666c6563744c455f6f665f6164644c6566745265666c6563744c54}\paperlink{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745265666c6563744c455f6f665f6164644c6566745265666c6563744c54}{P22.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745265666c6563744c455f6f665f6164644c6566745265666c6563744c54}\textbf{Sum left reflect at most of sum left reflect less than.} Let \(N\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add}\left(N\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Is\,Left\,Cancel\,Add}\left(N\right)\). Equip \(N\) with a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Reflect\,LT}\left(N\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Reflect\,LE}\left(N\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f69642e746f4164644c6566744d6f6e6f}\paperlink{premise:49734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f69642e746f4164644c6566744d6f6e6f}{P23.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f69642e746f4164644c6566744d6f6e6f}\textbf{To sum left mono.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Assume \(\text{translation preserves order on }\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Mono}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}\paperlink{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}{P24.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}\textbf{To is cancel sum.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Assume \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}\paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}{P25.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}\textbf{No zero divisors.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a semiring structure. Equip \(R\) with a linear order. Assume \(R\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Exists\,Add\,Of\,LE}\left(R\right)\). Then \(\operatorname{No\,Zero\,Divisors}\left(R\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}\paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}{P26.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}\textbf{To positivity product strict mono.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a semiring structure. Equip \(R\) with a partial order. Assume \(R\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\). Then \(\operatorname{Pos\,Mul\,Strict\,Mono}\left(R\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}{P27.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}\textbf{Is nat of nat.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Monoid\,With\,One}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{cast}_{\alpha}\left(n\right) = a\). Then \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772}{P28.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f636f6e6772}\textbf{Sum congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} + b^{'} = c\). Then \(a + b = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}{P29.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}\textbf{Sum product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{1}\), \(a_{2}\), \(b\), \(c_{1}\), \(c_{2}\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{1} \cdot b = c_{1}\). Assume \(a_{2} \cdot b = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} + c_{2} = d\). Then \(\left(a_{1} + a_{2}\right) \cdot b = d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}{P30.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}\textbf{Sum pf sum greater than.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{2}\), \(c\), and \(b_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a + b_{2} = c\). Then \(a + \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = b_{1} + c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}{P31.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}\textbf{Sum pf sum less than.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{2}\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(a_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a_{2} + b = c\). Then \(a_{1} + a_{2} + b = a_{1} + c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{P32.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}\textbf{Sum pf sum zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a + 0 = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}{P33.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}\textbf{Sum pf zero sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(0 + b = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{P34.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}\textbf{Atom pf.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a = a^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}{P35.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}\textbf{Cast positivity.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Assume \(\operatorname{Is\,Nat}\left(a, n\right)\). Then \(a = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(n\right) + 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}{P36.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}\textbf{Product sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{1}\), \(b_{2}\), \(c_{1}\), \(c_{2}\), and \(d\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a \cdot b_{1} = c_{1}\). Assume \(a \cdot b_{2} = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} + 0 + c_{2} = d\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b_{1} + b_{2}\right) = d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}{P37.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Product congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), \(b\), \(b^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \(a^{'} \cdot b^{'} = c\). Then \(a \cdot b = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}{P38.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}\textbf{Product pf left.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{3}\), \(b\), \(c\), and \(a_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(a_{2}\) be a natural number. Assume \(a_{3} \cdot b = c\). Then \(a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot a_{3} \cdot b = a_{1}^{a_{2}} \cdot c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}{P39.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}\textbf{Product pf right.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(b_{3}\), \(c\), and \(b_{1}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b_{2}\) be a natural number. Assume \(a \cdot b_{3} = c\). Then \(a \cdot \left(b_{1}^{b_{2}} \cdot b_{3}\right) = b_{1}^{b_{2}} \cdot c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f706f77}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f706f77}{P40.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f706f77}\textbf{Product power.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a_{2}\) and \(c_{2}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(e\), \(b\), and \(c_{1}\) be natural numbers. Let \(x\) be an element of \(R\). Assume \(e \cdot b = c_{1}\). Assume \(a_{2}^{b} = c_{2}\). Then \({\left(x^{e} \cdot a_{2}\right)}^{b} = x^{c_{1}} \cdot c_{2}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}{P41.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}\textbf{Product zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a \cdot 0 = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}{P42.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}\textbf{Of equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a = c\). Assume \(b = c\). Then \(a = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}{P43.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}\textbf{One product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) \cdot a = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f706f77}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f706f77}{P44.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f706f77}\textbf{One power.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(b\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)^{b} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f616464}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f616464}{P45.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f616464}\textbf{Power sum.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(c_{1}\), and \(c_{2}\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b_{1}\) and \(b_{2}\) be natural numbers. Let \(d\) be an element of \(R\). Assume \(a^{b_{1}} = c_{1}\). Assume \(a^{b_{2}} = c_{2}\). Assume \(c_{1} \cdot c_{2} = d\). Then \(a^{b_{1} + b_{2}} = d\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f636f6e6772}{P46.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f636f6e6772}\textbf{Power congruence.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\), \(a^{'}\), and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b\) and \(b^{'}\) be natural numbers. Assume \(a = a^{'}\). Assume \(b = b^{'}\). Assume \({a^{'}}^{b^{'}} = c\). Then \(a^{b} = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f7a65726f}{P47.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f7a65726f}\textbf{Power zero.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(a^{0} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e73696e676c655f706f77}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e73696e676c655f706f77}{P48.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e73696e676c655f706f77}\textbf{Single power.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(a\) and \(c\) be elements of \(R\). Let \(b\) be a natural number. Assume \(a^{b} = c\). Then \(\left(a + 0\right)^{b} = c + 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}{P49.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}\textbf{Zero product.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Let \(b\) be an element of \(R\). Then \(0 \cdot b = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e674e462e6e61745f726177436173745f31}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e674e462e6e61745f726177436173745f31}{P50.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e674e462e6e61745f726177436173745f31}\textbf{Nat raw cast 1.} Let \(R\) be a type. Equip \(R\) with a commutative semiring structure. Then \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}{P51.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}\textbf{Product zero.} Let \(M_{0}\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{self}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,Zero\,Class}\left(M_{0}\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M_{0}\). Then \(a \cdot 0 = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e7a65726f5f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e7a65726f5f6d756c}{P52.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e7a65726f5f6d756c}\textbf{Zero product.} Let \(M_{0}\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{self}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,Zero\,Class}\left(M_{0}\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M_{0}\). Then \(0 \cdot a = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{P53.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}\textbf{Count equals zero of not membership.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a multiset of elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \notin s\). Then \(\operatorname{count}\left(a, s\right) = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c6963617465}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c6963617465}{P54.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c6963617465}\textbf{Count replicate.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{count}(a,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(n,\allowbreak{} b)) = \begin{cases}n &\text{if } b = a\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c69636174655f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c69636174655f73656c66}{P55.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c69636174655f73656c66}\textbf{Count replicate self.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{count}\left(a, \operatorname{replicate}\left(n, a\right)\right) = n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d61705f7265706c6963617465}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d61705f7265706c6963617465}{P56.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d61705f7265706c6963617465}\textbf{Map replicate.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Let \(k\) be a natural number. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{map}(f,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(k,\allowbreak{} a)) = \operatorname{replicate}(k,\allowbreak{} f(a))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f6d6170}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f6d6170}{P57.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f6d6170}\textbf{Membership map.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Let \(b\) be an element of \(\beta\). Let \(s\) be a multiset of elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(b \in \operatorname{map}(f,\allowbreak{} s) \iff (\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s \land f(a) = b)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f746f46696e736574}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f746f46696e736574}{P58.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f746f46696e736574}\textbf{Membership to finset.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a multiset of elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(s\right) \iff a \in s\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f7a65726f}{P59.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f7a65726f}\textbf{Not membership zero.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \notin 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e70726f645f7265706c6963617465}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e70726f645f7265706c6963617465}{P60.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e70726f645f7265706c6963617465}\textbf{Product replicate.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Monoid}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}\left(\operatorname{replicate}\left(n, a\right)\right) = a^{n}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e73756d5f7265706c6963617465}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e73756d5f7265706c6963617465}{P61.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e73756d5f7265706c6963617465}\textbf{Sum replicate.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure. Let \(n\) be a natural number. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(\operatorname{replicate}(n,\allowbreak{} a)) = \sum_{k=1}^{n}(a)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d756c74697365742e746f46696e7365745f7265706c6963617465}\paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e746f46696e7365745f7265706c6963617465}{P62.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d756c74697365742e746f46696e7365745f7265706c6963617465}\textbf{To finset replicate.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(n\) be a natural number. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{to\,Finset}(\operatorname{replicate}(n,\allowbreak{} a)) = \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } n = 0\\ \{a\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f63686f6f73655f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c5f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f63686f6f73655f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c5f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c}{P63.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f63686f6f73655f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c5f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c}\textbf{Sum choose product factorial product factorial.} Let \(i\) and \(j\) be natural numbers. Then \(\binom{i + j}{j} \cdot i! \cdot j! = \left(i + j\right)!\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f6d756c5f616464}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f6d756c5f616464}{P64.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f6d756c5f616464}\textbf{Choose product sum.} Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n \ne 0\). Then \(\binom{m \cdot n + n}{n} = \left(m + 1\right) \cdot \binom{m \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}{P65.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}\textbf{Choose self.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\binom{n}{n} = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f7a65726f5f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f7a65726f5f7269676874}{P66.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f7a65726f5f7269676874}\textbf{Choose zero right.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\binom{n}{0} = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6469765f65715f6f665f65715f6d756c5f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6469765f65715f6f665f65715f6d756c5f6c656674}{P67.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6469765f65715f6f665f65715f6d756c5f6c656674}\textbf{Div equals of equals product left.} Let \(n\), \(m\), and \(k\) be natural numbers. Assume \(0 < n\). Assume \(m = k \cdot n\). Then \(m / n = k\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6469765f6d756c5f63616e63656c}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6469765f6d756c5f63616e63656c}{P68.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6469765f6d756c5f63616e63656c}\textbf{Div product cancel.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n \mid m\). Then \(m / n \cdot n = m\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6476645f6d756c5f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6476645f6d756c5f6c656674}{P69.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6476645f6d756c5f6c656674}\textbf{Divisibility product left.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be natural numbers. Then \(a \mid b \cdot a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}{P70.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}\textbf{Factorial positivity.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(0 < n!\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f73756363}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f73756363}{P71.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f73756363}\textbf{Factorial successor.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\left(n + 1\right)! = \left(n + 1\right) \cdot n!\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f7a65726f}{P72.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f7a65726f}\textbf{Factorial zero.} \(0! = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{P73.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}\textbf{Inst canonically ordered sum.} \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f6964}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f6964}{P74.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f6964}\textbf{Inst is ordered sum monoid.} \(\text{translation preserves order on }\mathbb{N}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}{P75.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}\textbf{Inst is ordered cancel sum monoid.} \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }\mathbb{N}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}{P76.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}\textbf{Strict order on the natural numbers.} \(\mathbb{N}\text{ is a strictly ordered semiring}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e73744e6f6e7472697669616c}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744e6f6e7472697669616c}{P77.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e73744e6f6e7472697669616c}\textbf{Inst nontrivial.} \(\operatorname{Nontrivial}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{P78.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}\textbf{Inst ordered difference.} \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f6c6566745f696e6a}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f6c6566745f696e6a}{P79.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f6c6566745f696e6a}\textbf{Product left injectivity.} Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be natural numbers. Assume \(a \ne 0\). Then \(b \cdot a = c \cdot a \iff b = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f706f73}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f706f73}{P80.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f706f73}\textbf{Product positivity.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n > 0\). Assume \(m > 0\). Then \(n \cdot m > 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f72696768745f696e6a}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f72696768745f696e6a}{P81.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f72696768745f696e6a}\textbf{Product right injectivity.} Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be natural numbers. Assume \(a \ne 0\). Then \(a \cdot b = a \cdot c \iff b = c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f656d707479}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f656d707479}{P82.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f656d707479}\textbf{Multinomial empty.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathbb{N}\). Then \(\operatorname{multinomial}\left(\varnothing, f\right) = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73696e676c65746f6e}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73696e676c65746f6e}{P83.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Multinomial singleton.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathbb{N}\). Then \(\operatorname{multinomial}\left(\{a\}, f\right) = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73706563}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73706563}{P84.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73706563}\textbf{Multinomial spec.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathbb{N}\). Then \(\prod_{i\in s}(f(i)!) \cdot \operatorname{multinomial}(s,\allowbreak{} f) = (\sum_{i\in s}(f(i)))!\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e706f735f6966665f6e655f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e706f735f6966665f6e655f7a65726f}{P85.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e706f735f6966665f6e655f7a65726f}\textbf{Positivity if and only if differs from zero.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(0 < n \iff n \ne 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e706f775f706f73}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e706f775f706f73}{P86.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e706f775f706f73}\textbf{Power positivity.} Let \(a\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. If \(0 < a\), then \(0 < a^{n}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e652e73796d6d}\paperlink{premise:4e652e73796d6d}{P87.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e652e73796d6d}\textbf{Symm.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \ne b\). Then \(b \ne a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}\paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{P88.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}\textbf{Inst subsingleton.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Sort}\). Let \(\mathit{inst}\) be a proof of \(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; \beta\left(a\right)\text{ has at most one element}\). Then \(\left(\Pi_{a\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(a\right)\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}\paperlink{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}{P89.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}\textbf{Elim.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Assume \(\alpha\text{ has at most one element}\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f7269676874}{P90.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f7269676874}\textbf{Sum tsub cancel right.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a partial order. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Comm\,Semigroup}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Reflect\,LE}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a + b - b = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6164645f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{P91.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f7a65726f}\textbf{Sum zero.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Zero\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(a + 0 = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:616e645f696d70}\paperlink{premise:616e645f696d70}{P92.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:616e645f696d70}\textbf{And imp.} Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be propositions. Then \(a \land b \implies c \iff a \implies b \implies c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:636f6e67725f617267}\paperlink{premise:636f6e67725f617267}{P93.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:636f6e67725f617267}\textbf{Alias.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a sort. Let \(a_{1}\) and \(a_{2}\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Assume \(a_{1} = a_{2}\). Then \(f\left(a_{1}\right) = f\left(a_{2}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:636f6e67725f617267e28282}\paperlink{premise:636f6e67725f617267e28282}{P94.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:636f6e67725f617267e28282}\textbf{Alias.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a sort. Let \(\gamma\) be a sort. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta \to \gamma\). Let \(x\) and \(x^{'}\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(y\) and \(y^{'}\) be elements of \(\beta\). Assume \(x = x^{'}\). Assume \(y = y^{'}\). Then \(f\left(x, y\right) = f\left(x^{'}, y^{'}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:636f6e74726176617269616e745f6c745f6f665f636f76617269616e745f6c65}\paperlink{premise:636f6e74726176617269616e745f6c745f6f665f636f76617269616e745f6c65}{P95.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:636f6e74726176617269616e745f6c745f6f665f636f76617269616e745f6c65}\textbf{Contravariant less than of covariant at most.} Let \(N\) be a type. Let \(\mathit{mu}\) be a function of type \(N \to N \to N\). Equip \(N\) with a linear order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Covariant\,Class}(N,\allowbreak{} N,\allowbreak{} \mathit{mu},\allowbreak{} \mathit{x1} \mapsto \mathit{x2} \mapsto \mathit{x1} \le \mathit{x2})\). Then \(\operatorname{Contravariant\,Class}(N,\allowbreak{} N,\allowbreak{} \mathit{mu},\allowbreak{} \mathit{x1} \mapsto \mathit{x2} \mapsto \mathit{x1} < \mathit{x2})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f636f6d6d}\paperlink{premise:65715f636f6d6d}{P96.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f636f6d6d}\textbf{Equals commutation.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a = b \iff b = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f66616c7365}\paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{P97.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f66616c7365}\textbf{Equals false.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(\neg p\). Then \(p = \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f6f665f686571}\paperlink{premise:65715f6f665f686571}{P98.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f6f665f686571}\textbf{Equals of heq.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\) and \(a^{'}\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(\left(a\, :\, \alpha\right)\text{ is heterogeneously equal to }\left(a^{'}\, :\, \alpha\right)\). Then \(a = a^{'}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:65715f73656c66}{P99.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f73656c66}\textbf{Equals self.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left(a = a\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:666f72616c6c5f6170706c795f65715f696d705f696666e28282}\paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f6170706c795f65715f696d705f696666e28282}{P100.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:666f72616c6c5f6170706c795f65715f696d705f696666e28282}\textbf{Forall apply equals imp iff2.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a sort. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(q\) be a function of type \(\beta \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \((\forall b\, :\, \beta,\; \forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a) \implies f(a) = b \implies q(b)) \iff (\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a) \implies q(f(a)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}{P101.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Forall congruence.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) and \(q\) be functions of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Assume \(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right) = q\left(a\right)\). Then \((\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a)) = (\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; q(a))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:666f72616c6c5f6578697374735f696e646578}\paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f6578697374735f696e646578}{P102.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:666f72616c6c5f6578697374735f696e646578}\textbf{Forall exists index.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(q\) be a function of type \(\left(\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(x\right)\right) \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \((\forall h\, :\, \exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p(x),\; q(h)) \iff (\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; \forall h\, :\, p(x),\; q(\operatorname{intro}(x,\allowbreak{} h)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:66756e657874}\paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{P103.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:66756e657874}\textbf{Function extensionality.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Sort}\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\Pi_{x\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(x\right)\). Let \(g\) be a function of type \(\Pi_{x\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(x\right)\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(f = g\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:67745f6966665f6c74}\paperlink{premise:67745f6966665f6c74}{P104.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:67745f6966665f6c74}\textbf{Greater than if and only if less than.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{LT}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(x\) and \(y\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(x > y \iff y < x\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:69665f6e6567}\paperlink{premise:69665f6e6567}{P105.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:69665f6e6567}\textbf{If neg.} Let \(c\) be a proposition. Let \(h\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(c\right)\). Assume \(\neg c\). Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(t\) and \(e\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(\begin{cases}t &\text{if } c\\ e &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} = e\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:69665f706f73}\paperlink{premise:69665f706f73}{P106.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:69665f706f73}\textbf{If positivity.} Let \(c\) be a proposition. Let \(h\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(c\right)\). Assume \(c\). Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(t\) and \(e\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(\begin{cases}t &\text{if } c\\ e &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} = t\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}{P107.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}\textbf{Implies congruence.} Let \(p_{1}\) and \(p_{2}\) be sorts. Let \(q_{1}\) and \(q_{2}\) be sorts. Assume \(p_{1} = p_{2}\). Assume \(q_{1} = q_{2}\). Then \(\left(p_{1} \to q_{1}\right) = \left(p_{2} \to q_{2}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}\paperlink{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}{P108.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}\textbf{Inst subsingleton decidable.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(p\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}\paperlink{premise:6974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}{P109.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}\textbf{Ite cond equals true.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(c\) be a proposition. Let \(x\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(c\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(c = \mathrm{True}\). Then \(\begin{cases}a &\text{if } c\\ b &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6974655f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:6974655f636f6e6772}{P110.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6974655f636f6e6772}\textbf{Ite congruence.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(b\) and \(c\) be propositions. Let \(x\), \(y\), \(u\), and \(v\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(b\right)\). Assume \(c\) is decidable. Assume \(b = c\). Assume \(c \implies x = u\). Assume \(\neg c \implies y = v\). Then \(\begin{cases}x &\text{if } b\\ y &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} = \begin{cases}u &\text{if } c\\ v &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6c655f7265666c}\paperlink{premise:6c655f7265666c}{P111.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6c655f7265666c}\textbf{At most refl.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \le a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}\paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{P112.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}\textbf{Multiplication associativity.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Semigroup}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(a \cdot b \cdot c = a \cdot \left(b \cdot c\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}\paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{P113.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}\textbf{Multiplication commutativity.} Let \(G\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Comm\,Magma}\left(G\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(G\). Then \(a \cdot b = b \cdot a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6d756c5f697465}\paperlink{premise:6d756c5f697465}{P114.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6d756c5f697465}\textbf{Product ite.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(P\) be a proposition. Assume \(P\) is decidable. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{1}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \cdot \begin{cases}b &\text{if } P\\ c &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} = \begin{cases}a \cdot b &\text{if } P\\ a \cdot c &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{P115.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}\textbf{Product one.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,One\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(a \cdot 1 = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e655f6f665f6774}\paperlink{premise:6e655f6f665f6774}{P116.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e655f6f665f6774}\textbf{Differs from of greater than.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(b < a\). Then \(a \ne b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}\paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{P117.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}\textbf{Not false equals true.} \(\left(\neg \mathrm{False}\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6f665f65715f74727565}\paperlink{premise:6f665f65715f74727565}{P118.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6f665f65715f74727565}\textbf{Of equals true.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(p = \mathrm{True}\). Then \(p\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6f6e655f6d756c}\paperlink{premise:6f6e655f6d756c}{P119.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6f6e655f6d756c}\textbf{One product.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Mul\,One\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(1 \cdot a = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6f6e655f706f77}\paperlink{premise:6f6e655f706f77}{P120.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6f6e655f706f77}\textbf{One power.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(1^{n} = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:706f775f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:706f775f6f6e65}{P121.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:706f775f6f6e65}\textbf{Power one.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(a^{1} = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:706f775f73756363}\paperlink{premise:706f775f73756363}{P122.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:706f775f73756363}\textbf{Power successor.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(a^{n + 1} = a^{n} \cdot a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:706f775f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:706f775f7a65726f}{P123.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:706f775f7a65726f}\textbf{Power zero.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Monoid}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(a^{0} = 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:747375625f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6c65}\paperlink{premise:747375625f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6c65}{P124.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:747375625f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6c65}\textbf{Alias.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \le b\). Then \(a - b = 0\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:7a65726f5f616464}\paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f616464}{P125.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:7a65726f5f616464}\textbf{Zero sum.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Zero\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(0 + a = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}\paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}{P126.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}\textbf{Zero tsub.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a commutative additive monoid structure and a partial order. Let \(\mathit{inst}_{2}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\alpha\right)\). Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}_{3}\) of type \(\operatorname{Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(\mathit{inst}_{4}\) be a proof of \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\alpha\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(0 - a = 0\).
\end{description}

\section{Multiset}
\begin{theorem}\label{theorem:1}
\(\operatorname{bell}\left(0\right) = 1\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{bell}\left(0\right) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{Factorial product for prescribed block sizes}]\label{theorem:2}
Let \(m\) be a multiset of elements of \(\mathbb{N}\). Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{bell}(m) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!.\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(i\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(i \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}} with \(n = i \cdot \operatorname{count}\left(i, m\right)\), we obtain \(0 < \left(i \cdot \operatorname{count}\left(i, m\right)\right)!\).
\end{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\} = \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right) \setminus \{0\}\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Proceed by induction on \(\operatorname{count}\left(x, m\right)\).
\emph{Base case.}
\begin{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:706f775f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:706f775f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}} and \paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}} gives \(x!^{0} \cdot 1 \cdot 1 = \left(x \cdot 0\right)!\).
\end{proofstep}
\emph{Successor step.}
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(c\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume as induction hypothesis that \(x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c}\left(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}\right) = \left(x \cdot c\right)!\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Equality 1.}\enspace
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!\), we obtain \(x! = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = c\), we obtain \(c = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b_{1} = c\) and \(b_{2} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f706f77}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f706f77}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f706f77}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f706f77}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \({(x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))}^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e73696e676c655f706f77}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e73696e676c655f706f77}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \({(x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0)}^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\), we obtain \({(x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0)}^{0} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a_{1} = x!\) and \(a_{2} = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot 0 = 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0 + 0 = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot (\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\), we obtain \(0 \cdot \left(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\right) = 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0 + 0 = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) \cdot (\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \({(x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0)}^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0} = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e706f775f636f6e6772}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(x!^{c} = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!\), we obtain \(x! = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a_{1} = x!\) and \(a_{2} = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b_{1} = x!\) and \(b_{2} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot 0 = 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + 0\), we obtain \(x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + 0 + 0 = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\), we obtain \(0 \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = 0\).

Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:706f775f73756363}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:706f775f73756363}} in the preceding result gives \(x!^{c + 1} \cdot ((c + 1) \cdot c!) \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c + 1}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = x! \cdot (c + 1) \cdot x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c + 1}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f73756363}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f73756363}} in the preceding result gives \(x!^{c + 1} \cdot (c + 1)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c + 1}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = x! \cdot (c + 1) \cdot x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c + 1}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Equality 2.}\enspace
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!\), we obtain \(x! = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = c\), we obtain \(c = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\).
By definition, we obtain \(1 = 1\).

By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\), we obtain \(c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0 + 0 = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).

By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).

By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + 0\), we obtain \(x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + 0 + 0 = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + 0\).
