By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot x! \cdot (c + 1) = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot ((\prod_{0\le x_{2}<c}(\binom{x_{2} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}))^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))))) + (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot ((\prod_{0\le x_{2}<c}(\binom{x_{2} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}))^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))))) + 0)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(x! \cdot (c + 1) \cdot x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot (\prod_{0\le x_{2}<c}(\binom{x_{2} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot x! \cdot (c + 1)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f72616e67655f73756363}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f72616e67655f73756363}} (the final factor at index \(c\) is \(\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}\)) in the preceding result gives \(x! \cdot (c + 1) \cdot x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c + 1}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot x! \cdot (c + 1)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Equality 3.}\enspace
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e61746f6d5f7066}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = \left(x \cdot c\right)!\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)! = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0\).

By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + 0\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + 0 + 0 = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f6e655f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a_{1} = c\) and \(a_{2} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) = c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f7269676874}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b_{1} = x!\) and \(b_{2} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a_{1} = \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}\) and \(a_{2} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \(\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) = \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f70665f6c656674}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a_{1} = \left(x \cdot c\right)!\) and \(a_{2} = \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) \cdot 0 = 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0 + 0 = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b = x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}\left(1\right) + 0\), we obtain \(0 \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0 + 0 = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + 0) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(b = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\), we obtain \(0 + ((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0) = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a_{1} = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + 0 + ((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0) = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + ((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f6d756c}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)) + ((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + 0)) \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1) + 0) = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + ((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6d756c5f636f6e6772}} with \(R = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((c + 1) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot (x \cdot c)! \cdot x! = (x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1))) + ((x \cdot c)!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (\binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (x!^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot (c^{\operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)} \cdot \operatorname{raw\,Cast}(1)))) + 0)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6f665f6571}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((x \cdot c)! \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot x! \cdot (c + 1) = (c + 1) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot (x \cdot c)! \cdot x!\).
Rewriting with \(x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c}\left(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}\right) = \left(x \cdot c\right)!\) in the preceding result gives \(x!^{c} \cdot c! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot x! \cdot (c + 1) = (c + 1) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot (x \cdot c)! \cdot x!\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Equality 4.}\enspace

\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e7a65726f5f6d756c}}, \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6774}}, \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e674e462e6e61745f726177436173745f31}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e674e462e6e61745f726177436173745f31}}, \paperlink{premise:706f775f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:706f775f6f6e65}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}}, \paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e636173745f706f73}}, \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e4d6574612e4e6f726d4e756d2e69734e61745f6f664e6174}}, \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f6164645f6c74}} and \paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e52696e672e6164645f70665f7a65726f5f616464}} gives \(\left(x \cdot c + x\right)! = \left(x \cdot \left(c + 1\right)\right)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f63686f6f73655f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c5f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6164645f63686f6f73655f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c5f6d756c5f666163746f7269616c}} in the preceding result gives \(\binom{x \cdot c + x}{x} \cdot \left(x \cdot c\right)! \cdot x! = \left(x \cdot \left(c + 1\right)\right)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}} in the preceding result gives \(\binom{c \cdot x + x}{x} \cdot \left(x \cdot c\right)! \cdot x! = \left(x \cdot \left(c + 1\right)\right)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f6d756c5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f6d756c5f616464}}, \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:416e642e6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:416e642e6c656674}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}}, \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f746f46696e736574}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f746f46696e736574}}, \(x \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right) \setminus \{0\}\) and \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}} in the preceding result gives \((c + 1) \cdot \binom{c \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} \cdot (x \cdot c)! \cdot x! = (x \cdot (c + 1))!\).
\par Combining these equalities gives \(x!^{c + 1} \cdot (c + 1)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<c + 1}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = (x \cdot (c + 1))!\).
\end{proofstep}
By induction, \(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)} \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = (x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m))!\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}} and the identity established above gives \(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)} \cdot (\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = (x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m))!\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}} with \(\iota = \mathbb{N}\) and \(M = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)} \cdot (\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}))) = \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}((x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c5f64697374726962}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c5f64697374726962}} in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}((x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Distinguish whether \(0 \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\) holds.
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(0 \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\).
Use the previously proved fact \(\prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}((x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6f6e655f706f77}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6f6e655f706f77}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}} and \paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e7a65726f5f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e7a65726f5f6d756c}} in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot 0!^{\operatorname{count}(0,\allowbreak{} m)} \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}((x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot (0 \cdot \operatorname{count}(0,\allowbreak{} m))!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f65726173655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f65726173655f6d756c}} (using \(0 \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\)) in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot 0!^{\operatorname{count}(0,\allowbreak{} m)} \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f65726173655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f65726173655f6d756c}} (using \(0 \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\)) in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{m_{1}\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(m_{1}!^{\operatorname{count}(m_{1},\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
\end{proofstep}
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(0 \notin \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\).
Use the previously proved fact \(\prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(x!^{\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}((x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}} (using \(0 \notin \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\)) in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{m_{1}\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(m_{1}!^{\operatorname{count}(m_{1},\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}} (using \(0 \notin \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\)) in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{m_{1}\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(m_{1}!^{\operatorname{count}(m_{1},\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
\end{proofstep}
The proposition or its negation holds. Hence \(\prod_{m_{1}\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(m_{1}!^{\operatorname{count}(m_{1},\allowbreak{} m)}) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c74697365745f6d61705f636f756e74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c74697365745f6d61705f636f756e74}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{x\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)! \cdot \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c5f64697374726962}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f6d756c5f64697374726962}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot (\prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!) \cdot \prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1}))) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!) \cdot \prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}} in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) \cdot (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!)) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}} with \(G = \mathbb{N}\) and \(\operatorname{count}\left(x, m\right)\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = x\), we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(x, m\right) \cdot x = x \cdot \operatorname{count}\left(x, m\right)\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f636f6e6772}} and the identity established above gives \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m)) = \sum_{m_{1}\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(\operatorname{count}(m_{1},\allowbreak{} m) \cdot m_{1})\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f6d756c74697365745f636f756e74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e73756d5f6d756c74697365745f636f756e74}} in the preceding result gives \(\sum_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m)) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)\).
By \paperlink{premise:636f6e67725f617267}{\emph{Alias}} (\paperlink{premise:636f6e67725f617267}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:636f6e67725f617267}}) with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(\beta = \mathbb{N}\) and \(f = \operatorname{factorial}\), we obtain \((\sum_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m)))! = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
By \paperlink{premise:636f6e67725f617267e28282}{\emph{Alias}} (\paperlink{premise:636f6e67725f617267e28282}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:636f6e67725f617267e28282}}) with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(\beta = \mathbb{N}\) and \(\gamma = \mathbb{N}\) and \(f = x \mapsto y \mapsto x \cdot y\), we obtain \(\prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) \cdot (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!)) \cdot (\sum_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m)))! = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}} in the preceding result gives \((\sum_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m)))! \cdot (\prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) \cdot (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!))) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}} in the preceding result gives \((\sum_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}(i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m)))! \cdot \prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73706563}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73706563}} in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot \operatorname{multinomial}(\operatorname{to\,Finset}(m),\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}} in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot (\operatorname{multinomial}(\operatorname{to\,Finset}(m),\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{k\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} m)}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!)) = \prod_{i\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m)}((i \cdot \operatorname{count}(i,\allowbreak{} m))!) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f72696768745f696e6a}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f72696768745f696e6a}}, \paperlink{premise:6e655f6f665f6774}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e655f6f665f6774}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f706f73}}, \paperlink{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f5a65726f4c654f6e65436c617373}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f5a65726f4c654f6e65436c617373}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, \paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744e6f6e7472697669616c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744e6f6e7472697669616c}} and the auxiliary result established above in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{bell}(m) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{Factorial quotient for prescribed block sizes}]\label{theorem:3}
Let \(m\) be a multiset of elements of \(\mathbb{N}\). Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{bell}(m) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)! / (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!)).\)
\end{center}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f6d6170}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e6d656d5f6d6170}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(\beta = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(x \in \operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m) \iff (\exists a\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; a \in m \land (j \mapsto j!)(a) = x)\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((x \in \operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) = (\exists a\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; a \in m \land a! = x)\).
By definition, we obtain \(\left(0 < x\right) = \left(0 < x\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}} with \(p_{1} = x \in \operatorname{map}\left(j \mapsto j!, m\right)\) and \(p_{2} = \exists a\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; a \in m \land a! = x\) and \(q_{1} = 0 < x\) and \(q_{2} = 0 < x\), we obtain \((x \in \operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m) \implies 0 < x) = ((\exists a\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; a \in m \land a! = x) \implies 0 < x)\).
By \paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f6578697374735f696e646578}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:666f72616c6c5f6578697374735f696e646578}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\forall h\, :\, \exists x_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; (x_{2} \mapsto x_{2} \in m \land x_{2}! = x)(x_{1}),\; (h_{1} \mapsto 0 < x)(h)) \iff (\forall x_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; \forall h\, :\, (x_{2} \mapsto x_{2} \in m \land x_{2}! = x)(x_{1}),\; (h_{1} \mapsto 0 < x)(\operatorname{intro}(x_{1},\allowbreak{} h)))\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \(((\exists x_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; x_{1} \in m \land x_{1}! = x) \implies 0 < x) = (\forall x_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; x_{1} \in m \land x_{1}! = x \implies 0 < x)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((x \in \operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m) \implies 0 < x) = (\forall x_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; x_{1} \in m \land x_{1}! = x \implies 0 < x)\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:616e645f696d70}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:616e645f696d70}}, we obtain \(x_{1} \in m \land x_{1}! = x \implies 0 < x \iff x_{1} \in m \implies x_{1}! = x \implies 0 < x\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((x_{1} \in m \land x_{1}! = x \implies 0 < x) = (x_{1} \in m \implies x_{1}! = x \implies 0 < x)\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\forall a\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; a \in m \land a! = x \implies 0 < x) = (\forall a\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; a \in m \implies a! = x \implies 0 < x)\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((x \in \operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m) \implies 0 < x) = (\forall a\, :\, \mathbb{N},\; a \in m \implies a! = x \implies 0 < x)\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in m\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}} with \(n = x\), we obtain \(0 < x!\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \operatorname{to\,Finset}\left(m\right) \setminus \{0\}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}} with \(n = \operatorname{count}\left(x, m\right)\), we obtain \(0 < \operatorname{count}\left(x, m\right)!\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{theorem:2}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:2}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{bell}(m) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{bell}(m) \cdot (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!)) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6469765f6d756c5f63616e63656c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6469765f6d756c5f63616e63656c}}, \paperlink{theorem:2}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:2}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6476645f6d756c5f6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6476645f6d756c5f6c656674}} and substitution of equals in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{bell}(m) \cdot (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!)) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)! / (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!)) \cdot (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!))\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f6c6566745f696e6a}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f6c6566745f696e6a}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e706f735f6966665f6e655f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e706f735f6966665f6e655f7a65726f}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f706f73}}, \paperlink{premise:67745f6966665f6c74}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:67745f6966665f6c74}}, \paperlink{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e6d756c74697365745f70726f645f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e6d756c74697365745f70726f645f706f73}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, \paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e6e6f5a65726f44697669736f7273}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737449735374726963744f72646572656452696e67}}, \paperlink{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f4578697374734164644f664c45}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f4578697374734164644f664c45}}, \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744e6f6e7472697669616c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744e6f6e7472697669616c}}, \paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f6170706c795f65715f696d705f696666e28282}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:666f72616c6c5f6170706c795f65715f696d705f696666e28282}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f706f73}}, \paperlink{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f5a65726f4c654f6e65436c617373}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:43616e6f6e6963616c6c794f7264657265644164642e746f5a65726f4c654f6e65436c617373}}, \paperlink{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49735374726963744f72646572656452696e672e746f506f734d756c5374726963744d6f6e6f}}, the identity established above and the two auxiliary results established above in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{bell}(m) = \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}sum}(m)! / (\operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} m)) \cdot \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(m) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} m)!))\).
\end{proof}

\section{Natural numbers}
\begin{lemma}[{Product formula for uniform Bell numbers}]\label{theorem:4}
Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}).\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Distinguish whether \(m = 0\) holds.
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(m = 0\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(k\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(a = a_{1}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{2}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Fix \(a_{3}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Assume \(a_{2} = a_{3}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{2}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{2}\right)\).
Use the hypothesis \(a_{2} = a_{3}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{2}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{3}\right)\).
\end{proofstep}
Use the hypothesis \(a = a_{1}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\forall a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; \forall a_{3}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; a_{2} = a_{3} \implies \operatorname{count}(a,\allowbreak{} a_{2}) = \operatorname{count}(a_{1},\allowbreak{} a_{3})\).
\end{proofstep}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}} with \(p = a = a_{1}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(a = a_{1}\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (b \mapsto \operatorname{Decidable}(a = b))(a_{1}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (a_{1} \mapsto \Pi_{b\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, \operatorname{Decidable}(a_{1} = b))(a))\text{ has at most one element}\).
By \paperlink{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Decidable\,Eq}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\) and \(a = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\) and \(b = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat} = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\).
By definition, we obtain \(k = k\).
Use the hypothesis \(m = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right) = \operatorname{replicate}\left(0, n\right)\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} 0)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f7a65726f}} and \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}} gives \(k \notin 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(k, 0\right) = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(k, \operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right)\right) = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(k \cdot \operatorname{count}\left(k, \operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right)\right) = k \cdot 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}} with \(M_{0} = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = k\), we obtain \(k \cdot 0 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(k \cdot \operatorname{count}\left(k, \operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right)\right) = 0\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \varnothing\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Fix \(a_{2}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(a_{1} = a_{2}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{3}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Fix \(a_{4}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Assume \(a_{3} = a_{4}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{3}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{3}\right)\).
Use the hypothesis \(a_{3} = a_{4}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{3}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{4}\right)\).
\end{proofstep}
Use the hypothesis \(a_{1} = a_{2}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\forall a_{3}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; \forall a_{4}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; a_{3} = a_{4} \implies \operatorname{count}(a_{1},\allowbreak{} a_{3}) = \operatorname{count}(a_{2},\allowbreak{} a_{4})\).
\end{proofstep}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{2}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}} with \(p = a_{1} = a_{2}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(a_{1} = a_{2}\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a_{2}\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (b \mapsto \operatorname{Decidable}(a_{1} = b))(a_{2}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (a_{2} \mapsto \Pi_{b\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, \operatorname{Decidable}(a_{2} = b))(a_{1}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
By \paperlink{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Decidable\,Eq}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\) and \(\operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat} = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\).
By definition, we obtain \(x = x\).
Use the hypothesis \(m = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right) = \operatorname{replicate}\left(0, n\right)\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} 0)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f7a65726f}} and \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}} gives \(x \notin 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(x, 0\right) = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(x, \operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right)\right) = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))\} = \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<0\}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x_{1} \in \varnothing\).
By definition, we obtain \(\binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} = \binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}} with \(\iota = \mathbb{N}\) and \(M = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = \prod_{x_{1}\in \varnothing}(\binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \varnothing\).
By definition, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = \binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{\emph{Function extensionality}} (\paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:66756e657874}}), \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f656d707479}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f656d707479}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}}, \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f656d707479}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f745f6d656d5f656d707479}}, \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}}, \(m = 0\) and the 3 identities established above gives \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\varnothing,\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{x\in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:69665f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:69665f706f73}} (using \(m = 0\)), \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}} and the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases},\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{k\in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\neg \left(m = 0\right)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{n\} \setminus \{0\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = \prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
Distinguish whether \(n = 0\) holds.
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(n = 0\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(k\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(a = a_{1}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{2}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Fix \(a_{3}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Assume \(a_{2} = a_{3}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{2}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{2}\right)\).
Use the hypothesis \(a_{2} = a_{3}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{2}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a, a_{3}\right)\).
\end{proofstep}
Use the hypothesis \(a = a_{1}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\forall a_{2}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; \forall a_{3}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; a_{2} = a_{3} \implies \operatorname{count}(a,\allowbreak{} a_{2}) = \operatorname{count}(a_{1},\allowbreak{} a_{3})\).
\end{proofstep}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}} with \(p = a = a_{1}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(a = a_{1}\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (b \mapsto \operatorname{Decidable}(a = b))(a_{1}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (a_{1} \mapsto \Pi_{b\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, \operatorname{Decidable}(a_{1} = b))(a))\text{ has at most one element}\).
By \paperlink{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Decidable\,Eq}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\) and \(a = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\) and \(b = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat} = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\).
By definition, we obtain \(k = k\).
Use the hypothesis \(n = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right) = \operatorname{replicate}\left(m, 0\right)\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} 0))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} 0))\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \varnothing\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Fix \(a_{2}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(a_{1} = a_{2}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{3}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Fix \(a_{4}\) in \(\operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N})\).
Assume \(a_{3} = a_{4}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{3}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{3}\right)\).
Use the hypothesis \(a_{3} = a_{4}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{3}\right) = \operatorname{count}\left(a_{1}, a_{4}\right)\).
\end{proofstep}
Use the hypothesis \(a_{1} = a_{2}\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\forall a_{3}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; \forall a_{4}\, :\, \operatorname{Multiset}(\mathbb{N}),\; a_{3} = a_{4} \implies \operatorname{count}(a_{1},\allowbreak{} a_{3}) = \operatorname{count}(a_{2},\allowbreak{} a_{4})\).
\end{proofstep}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{2}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e446563696461626c65}} with \(p = a_{1} = a_{2}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(a_{1} = a_{2}\right)\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a_{2}\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (b \mapsto \operatorname{Decidable}(a_{1} = b))(a_{2}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:50692e696e737453756273696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \((\Pi_{a_{1}\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, (a_{2} \mapsto \Pi_{b\, :\, \mathbb{N}}\, \operatorname{Decidable}(a_{2} = b))(a_{1}))\text{ has at most one element}\).
By \paperlink{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:53756273696e676c65746f6e2e656c696d}} with \(\alpha = \operatorname{Decidable\,Eq}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\) and \(\operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat} = \operatorname{inst\,Decidable\,Eq\,Nat}\).
By definition, we obtain \(x = x\).
Use the hypothesis \(n = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{replicate}\left(m, n\right) = \operatorname{replicate}\left(m, 0\right)\).
Substituting equals, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} 0))\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))\} = \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} 0))\}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x_{1} \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} 0))\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} = \binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}} with \(\iota = \mathbb{N}\) and \(M = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = \prod_{0\le x_{1}<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} 0))}(\binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<m\}\).
Use the hypothesis \(n = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(x \cdot n = x \cdot 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}} with \(M_{0} = \mathbb{N}\) and \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(x \cdot 0 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(x \cdot n = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\left(y \mapsto x \cdot n + y\right) = \left(y \mapsto 0 + y\right)\).
Use the hypothesis \(n = 0\).
By congruence, we obtain \(x \cdot n + n = 0 + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f7a65726f}} with \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(0\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(0 + 0 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(x \cdot n + n = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(x \cdot n + n - 1 = 0 - 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(0 - 1 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(x \cdot n + n - 1 = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{choose}\left(x \cdot n + n - 1\right) = \operatorname{choose}\left(0\right)\).
Use the hypothesis \(n = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(n - 1 = 0 - 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(0 - 1 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(n - 1 = 0\).
By congruence, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = \binom{0}{0}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}} with \(0\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \(\binom{0}{0} = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = 1\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \(n = 0\), \paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{\emph{Function extensionality}} (\paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:66756e657874}}), \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73696e676c65746f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f73696e676c65746f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}} and the 3 identities established above gives \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\{n\},\allowbreak{} x \mapsto x \cdot \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{x\in \{n\} \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\neg \left(n = 0\right)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:4e652e73796d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e652e73796d6d}} (using \(\neg \left(n = 0\right)\)) and \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}} gives \(\{n\} \setminus \{0\} = \{n\}\).
Use the previously proved fact \(\{n\} \setminus \{0\} = \{n\}\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(k\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c6963617465}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c6963617465}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = k\) and \(b = n\) and \(m\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \begin{cases}m &\text{if } n = k\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = k \cdot \begin{cases}m &\text{if } n = k\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f697465}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f697465}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(P = n = k\) and \(a = k\) and \(b = m\) and \(c = 0\), we obtain \(k \cdot \begin{cases}m &\text{if } n = k\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} = \begin{cases}k \cdot m &\text{if } n = k\\ k \cdot 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \begin{cases}k \cdot m &\text{if } n = k\\ k \cdot 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\left(n = k\right) = \left(n = k\right)\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(n = k\).
By definition, we obtain \(k \cdot m = k \cdot m\).
\end{proofstep}
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\neg \left(n = k\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}} with \(M_{0} = \mathbb{N}\) and \(k\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(k \cdot 0 = 0\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:6974655f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6974655f636f6e6772}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\begin{cases}k \cdot m &\text{if } n = k\\ k \cdot 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} = \begin{cases}k \cdot m &\text{if } n = k\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \begin{cases}k \cdot m &\text{if } n = k\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{n\}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c6963617465}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c6963617465}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = n\) and \(m\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \begin{cases}m &\text{if } n = x\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))\} = \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<\begin{cases}m &\text{if } n = x\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x_{1}\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x_{1} \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<\begin{cases}m &\text{if } n = x\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1} = \binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}} with \(\iota = \mathbb{N}\) and \(M = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1}) = \prod_{0\le x_{1}<\begin{cases}m &\text{if } n = x\\ 0 &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}}(\binom{x_{1} \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<m\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = \binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{\emph{Function extensionality}} (\paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:66756e657874}}), \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c74696e6f6d69616c5f73696e676c65746f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:6974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}}, \paperlink{premise:6f6e655f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6f6e655f6d756c}} and the 3 identities established above gives \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\{n\},\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{x\in \{n\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
Rewriting with the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\{n\},\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{x\in \{n\} \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
The proposition or its negation holds. Hence \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\{n\},\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{x\in \{n\} \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot x + x - 1}{x - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:69665f6e6567}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:69665f6e6567}} (using \(\neg \left(m = 0\right)\)), \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}} and the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases},\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{k\in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
\end{proofstep}
The proposition or its negation holds. Hence \(\operatorname{multinomial}(\begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases},\allowbreak{} k \mapsto k \cdot \operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) \cdot \prod_{k\in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}}(\prod_{0\le j<\operatorname{count}(k,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))}(\binom{j \cdot k + k - 1}{k - 1})) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e746f46696e7365745f7265706c6963617465}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e746f46696e7365745f7265706c6963617465}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{bell}(\operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) = \prod_{0\le p<m}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1})\).
By the preceding argument, we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, n\right) = \prod_{0\le p<m}\left(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\right)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{The value for zero blocks}]\label{theorem:5}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(0, n\right) = 1\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \paperlink{theorem:4}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:4}} with \(m = 0\), we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(0, n\right) = \prod_{0\le p<0}\left(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\right)\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(0,\allowbreak{} n) = 1) = (\prod_{0\le p<0}(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}) = 1)\).
By \paperlink{premise:65715f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f73656c66}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = 1\), we obtain \(\left(1 = 1\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\left(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(0, n\right) = 1\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6f665f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6f665f65715f74727565}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(0, n\right) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{The value for blocks of size zero}]\label{theorem:6}
Let \(m\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, 0\right) = 1\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<m\}\).
