By \paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e6d756c5f7a65726f}} with \(M_{0} = \mathbb{N}\) and \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(x \cdot 0 = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(x \cdot 0 + 0 = 0 + 0\).
By \paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f7a65726f}} with \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(0\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(0 + 0 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(x \cdot 0 + 0 = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(x \cdot 0 + 0 - 1 = 0 - 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(0 - 1 = 0\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(x \cdot 0 + 0 - 1 = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{choose}\left(x \cdot 0 + 0 - 1\right) = \operatorname{choose}\left(0\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:7a65726f5f74737562}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(1\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(0 - 1 = 0\).
By congruence, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot 0 + 0 - 1}{0 - 1} = \binom{0}{0}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}} with \(n = 0\), we obtain \(\binom{0}{0} = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot 0 + 0 - 1}{0 - 1} = 1\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:4}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:4}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}} and the identity established above gives \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, 0\right) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{Recurrence for one additional block}]\label{theorem:7}
Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m + 1,\allowbreak{} n) = \binom{m \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} \cdot \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n).\)
\end{center}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<m + 1\}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}} with \(G = \mathbb{N}\) and \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = n\), we obtain \(x \cdot n = n \cdot x\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = \binom{n \cdot x + n - 1}{n - 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<m\}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}} with \(G = \mathbb{N}\) and \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = n\), we obtain \(x \cdot n = n \cdot x\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = \binom{n \cdot x + n - 1}{n - 1}\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:4}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:4}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f72616e67655f73756363}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f72616e67655f73756363}} (the final factor at index \(m\) is \(\binom{n \cdot m + n - 1}{n - 1}\)), \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f636f6d6d}} and the two identities established above gives \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m + 1,\allowbreak{} n) = \binom{m \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} \cdot \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{The value for one block}]\label{theorem:8}
Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(1, n\right) = 1\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \paperlink{theorem:4}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:4}} with \(m = 1\), we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(1, n\right) = \prod_{0\le p<1}\left(\binom{p \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}} with \(\iota = \mathbb{N}\) and \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(f = x \mapsto \binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\) and \(a = 0\), we obtain \(\prod_{x\in \{0\}}\left(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\right) = \binom{0 \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e7a65726f5f6d756c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c5a65726f436c6173732e7a65726f5f6d756c}} with \(M_{0} = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = n\), we obtain \(0 \cdot n = 0\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(0 \cdot n + n = 0 + n\).
By \paperlink{premise:7a65726f5f616464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:7a65726f5f616464}} with \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = n\), we obtain \(0 + n = n\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(0 \cdot n + n = n\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\binom{0 \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = \binom{n - 1}{n - 1}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f73656c66}} with \(n - 1\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \(\binom{n - 1}{n - 1} = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\binom{0 \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1} = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\prod_{x\in \{0\}}\left(\binom{x \cdot n + n - 1}{n - 1}\right) = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(1, n\right) = 1\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\left(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(1, n\right) = 1\right) = \left(1 = 1\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:65715f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f73656c66}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(a = 1\), we obtain \(\left(1 = 1\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\left(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(1, n\right) = 1\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6f665f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6f665f65715f74727565}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(1, n\right) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{The value for singleton blocks}]\label{theorem:9}
Let \(m\) be a natural number. Then \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, 1\right) = 1\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{i\in\mathbb{N}\mid i<m\}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6f6e65}} with \(M = \mathbb{N}\) and \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\), we obtain \(x \cdot 1 = x\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(x \cdot 1 + 1 - 1 = x + 1 - 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f6964}}, we obtain \(\text{translation preserves and reflects order on }\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49734f72646572656443616e63656c4164644d6f6e6f69642e746f497343616e63656c416464}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Is\,Cancel\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:497343616e63656c4164642e746f49734c65667443616e63656c416464}} with \(G = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Is\,Left\,Cancel\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f6964}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737449734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f6964}}, we obtain \(\text{translation preserves order on }\mathbb{N}\).
By \paperlink{premise:49734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f69642e746f4164644c6566744d6f6e6f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49734f7264657265644164644d6f6e6f69642e746f4164644c6566744d6f6e6f}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Mono}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:636f6e74726176617269616e745f6c745f6f665f636f76617269616e745f6c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:636f6e74726176617269616e745f6c745f6f665f636f76617269616e745f6c65}} with \(N = \mathbb{N}\) and \(\mathit{mu} = \mathit{x1} \mapsto \mathit{x2} \mapsto \mathit{x1} + \mathit{x2}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Contravariant\,Class}(\mathbb{N},\allowbreak{} \mathbb{N},\allowbreak{} \mathit{x1} \mapsto \mathit{x2} \mapsto \mathit{x1} + \mathit{x2},\allowbreak{} \mathit{x1} \mapsto \mathit{x2} \mapsto \mathit{x1} < \mathit{x2})\).
By \paperlink{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745265666c6563744c455f6f665f6164644c6566745265666c6563744c54}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:49734c65667443616e63656c4164642e6164644c6566745265666c6563744c455f6f665f6164644c6566745265666c6563744c54}} with \(N = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(\operatorname{Add\,Left\,Reflect\,LE}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6164645f747375625f63616e63656c5f7269676874}} with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\) and \(x\) in place of its parameter \(a\) and \(b = 1\), we obtain \(x + 1 - 1 = x\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(x \cdot 1 + 1 - 1 = x\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \(\operatorname{choose}\left(x \cdot 1 + 1 - 1\right) = \operatorname{choose}\left(x\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e737443616e6f6e6963616c6c794f726465726564416464}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Canonically\,Ordered\,Add}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e696e73744f726465726564537562}}, we obtain \(\operatorname{Ordered\,Sub}\left(\mathbb{N}\right)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6c655f7265666c}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6c655f7265666c}} gives \(1 \le 1\).
By \paperlink{premise:747375625f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6c65}{\emph{Alias}} (\paperlink{premise:747375625f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6c65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:747375625f65715f7a65726f5f6f665f6c65}}) with \(\alpha = \mathbb{N}\), we obtain \(1 - 1 = 0\).
By congruence, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot 1 + 1 - 1}{1 - 1} = \binom{x}{0}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f7a65726f5f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e63686f6f73655f7a65726f5f7269676874}} with \(n = x\), we obtain \(\binom{x}{0} = 1\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \(\binom{x \cdot 1 + 1 - 1}{1 - 1} = 1\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:4}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:4}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e73745f6f6e65}} and the identity established above gives \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, 1\right) = 1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{Factorial product for uniform Bell numbers}]\label{theorem:10}
Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n \ne 0\). Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, n\right) \cdot n!^{m} \cdot m! = \left(m \cdot n\right)!.\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\operatorname{bell}(\operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) \cdot \operatorname{Multiset\mathord{:}prod}(\operatorname{map}(j \mapsto j!,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))) = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m}\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m} = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m}\).
Assume \(\text{the proof of }(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m} = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m})\text{ agrees under transport with the proof of }(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m} = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m})\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))! = \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!\).
\end{proofstep}
Distinguish whether \(m = 0\) holds.
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(m = 0\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))! = \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \(\varnothing \setminus \{0\} = \varnothing\).
Use the previously proved fact \(\varnothing \setminus \{0\} = \varnothing\).
\end{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \(1 = 1\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f656d707479}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f656d707479}} in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{x\in \varnothing}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(0,\allowbreak{} n))!) = 1\).
Rewriting with the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(\prod_{x\in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(0,\allowbreak{} n))!) = 1\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:65715f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f636f6d6d}} in the preceding result gives \(1 = \prod_{x\in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(0,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f7a65726f}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f7a65726f}} in the preceding result gives \(0! = \prod_{x\in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(0,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
Rewriting with \(m = 0\) in the preceding result gives \(m! = \prod_{x\in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:69665f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:69665f706f73}} (using \(m = 0\)) and the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(m! = \prod_{x\in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
\end{proofstep}
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\neg \left(m = 0\right)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \{n\} \setminus \{0\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))! = \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}}, \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f73696e676c65746f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f66616c7365}}, \paperlink{premise:4e652e73796d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e652e73796d6d}} (using \(n \ne 0\)) and \paperlink{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6e6f745f66616c73655f65715f74727565}} gives \(\{n\} \setminus \{0\} = \{n\}\).
Use the previously proved fact \(\{n\} \setminus \{0\} = \{n\}\).
\end{proofstep}
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f73696e676c65746f6e}} and \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c69636174655f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e636f756e745f7265706c69636174655f73656c66}} gives \(m! = \prod_{x\in \{n\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
Rewriting with the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(m! = \prod_{x\in \{n\} \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:69665f6e6567}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:69665f6e6567}} (using \(\neg \left(m = 0\right)\)) and the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(m! = \prod_{x\in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
\end{proofstep}
The proposition or its negation holds. Hence \(m! = \prod_{x\in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e70726f645f636f6e6772}}, \paperlink{premise:4d756c74697365742e746f46696e7365745f7265706c6963617465}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4d756c74697365742e746f46696e7365745f7265706c6963617465}} and the identity established above in the preceding result gives \(m! = \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(\operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(\operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!) = m!\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(m! = m!\).
Assume \(\text{the proof of }\left(m! = m!\right)\text{ agrees under transport with the proof of }\left(m! = m!\right)\).
By reflexivity, we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, n\right) \cdot n!^{m} \cdot m!\) heterogeneously equal to itself.
\end{proofstep}
Use the hypothesis \(\prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(\operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!) = m!\).
Reversing the equality, we obtain \(m! = \prod_{j\in \operatorname{to\,Finset}(\operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n)) \setminus \{0\}}(\operatorname{count}(j,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!)\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \begin{cases}\varnothing &\text{if } m = 0\\ \{n\} &\text{otherwise}\end{cases} \setminus \{0\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))! = \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!\).
\end{proofstep}
Distinguish whether \(m = 0\) holds.
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(m = 0\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x\) in \(\mathbb{N}\).
Assume \(x \in \varnothing \setminus \{0\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))! = \operatorname{count}(x,\allowbreak{} \operatorname{replicate}(m,\allowbreak{} n))!\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \(\varnothing \setminus \{0\} = \varnothing\).
Use the previously proved fact \(\varnothing \setminus \{0\} = \varnothing\).

\begin{theorem}[{Factorial quotient for uniform Bell numbers}]\label{theorem:11}
Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n \ne 0\). Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) = (m \cdot n)! / (n!^{m} \cdot m!).\)
\end{center}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}}, we obtain \(0 < n!\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e706f775f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e706f775f706f73}}, we obtain \(0 < n!^{m}\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e666163746f7269616c5f706f73}} with \(m\) in place of its parameter \(n\), we obtain \(0 < m!\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f706f73}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6d756c5f706f73}}, we obtain \(n!^{m} \cdot m! > 0\).
By definition, we obtain \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m} \cdot m! = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}(m,\allowbreak{} n) \cdot n!^{m} \cdot m!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{theorem:10}{Lemma~\ref{theorem:10}} (using \(n \ne 0\)) in the preceding result gives \(\left(m \cdot n\right)! = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, n\right) \cdot n!^{m} \cdot m!\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6d756c5f6173736f63}} in the preceding result gives \(\left(m \cdot n\right)! = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, n\right) \cdot \left(n!^{m} \cdot m!\right)\).
By \paperlink{premise:4e61742e6469765f65715f6f665f65715f6d756c5f6c656674}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4e61742e6469765f65715f6f665f65715f6d756c5f6c656674}}, we obtain \(\left(m \cdot n\right)! / \left(n!^{m} \cdot m!\right) = \operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, n\right)\).
Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:65715f636f6d6d}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:65715f636f6d6d}} in the preceding result gives \(\operatorname{uniform\,Bell}\left(m, n\right) = \left(m \cdot n\right)! / \left(n!^{m} \cdot m!\right)\).
\end{proof}
